researcher-0002:P1.1 [proved; polished] Component Measures
DEPS: KTZ Lemma 2.1; KTZ Lemma 6.2
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $d$ be an integer satisfying $100<d<p$. Let $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$, and assign to every affine line $L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_p^2$ a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$, where an incidence $(x,L)$ is accepted when $P_L(x)=f(x)$. Put

$$
t=|T|,\qquad \mu(X)=\frac{|X|}{p^2},\qquad \tau=\frac{t}{p(p+1)},\qquad C_*=2^{40}.
$$

Assume

$$
k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2.
$$

Then

$$
\mu(X)\ge\frac{k}{4p},\qquad \tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$

In particular, $t\ge k(p+1)/4$.

researcher-0002:P2.1 [proved; polished] Reaching Family
DEPS: P1; Chernoff bound; uniqueness of the affine line through two points
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of P1, there exists a set $R\subseteq T$ such that

$$
\frac{4t}{k}\le |R|\le\frac{16t}{k},
$$

and, for every $L\in T$,

$$
\left|\left\{x\in N_G(L):\exists\,M\in R\setminus\{L\}\text{ with }(x,M)\in E\right\}\right|
>\frac12\deg_G(L).
$$

Moreover, $|R|\ge p+1$ and $|R|=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$ with absolute implied constants.

researcher-0002:P3.1 [proved; polished] Trivariate Explainer
DEPS: P2; KTZ Lemmas 2.2, 2.3, and 2.5
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of P1 and P2, put $r=|R|$ and

$$
D=16\left(\left\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\right\rfloor+1\right).
$$

There exists a nonzero squarefree polynomial $A\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$ such that

$$
A_Z\not\equiv0
$$

and

$$
A(M(s),P_M(s))\equiv0\qquad\text{for every }M\in R,
$$

where $M(s)$ is an affine parametrization of $M$. Moreover,

$$
D^2>256dr,
\qquad
D^2\le1024dr\le\frac{16384dt}{k}\le\frac{32768d\tau p^2}{k},
\qquad
\frac{D}{k}<\frac1{5000},
$$

so $D<k\le p<r$ and $D=O\!\left(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k}\right)$.

researcher-0002:P4.1 [proved; polished] Linewise Extension
DEPS: P2; P3
STATEMENT: Let $G=(X,T,E)$, $R$, $A$, and $D$ satisfy the hypotheses and conclusions of P2 and P3. Then, for every $L\in T$ and every affine parametrization $L(s)$,

$$
A(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0002:P5.1 [proved; polished] Exceptional-Root Cleanup
DEPS: P4; KTZ Lemmas 2.4 and 2.6
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of P4, write

$$
A=\sum_j a_j(X,Y)Z^j,
\qquad
H(X,Y)=\gcd_j a_j(X,Y),
\qquad
B=A/H.
$$

Then $B$ is squarefree, has total degree less than $p$, and satisfies

$$
B(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0
$$

for every $L\in T$ such that $H(L(s))\not\equiv0$. There exists a formal directional derivative $\mathcal{D}$ such that

$$
\gcd(B,\mathcal{D}B)=1.
$$

Define

$$
T_{\mathrm{triv}}=\{L\in T:H(L(s))\equiv0\}
$$

and

$$
T_{\mathrm{deg}}=
\left\{L\in T\setminus T_{\mathrm{triv}}:(\mathcal{D}B)(L(s),P_L(s))\equiv0\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
|T_{\mathrm{triv}}|\le D,
\qquad
|T_{\mathrm{deg}}|\le D(D-1),
$$

and every $L\in T\setminus(T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}})$ satisfies

$$
\left|\{x\in N_G(L):(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\}\right|\le D.
$$

Consequently, for

$$
E_{\mathrm{exc}}=
\left\{(x,L)\in E:L\in T_{\mathrm{triv}}\cup T_{\mathrm{deg}}
\text{ or }(\mathcal{D}B)(x,f(x))=0\right\},
$$

one has

$$
|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|\le pD^2+Dt.
$$

researcher-0002:P6.1 [proved; polished] Clean Core
DEPS: P5
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of P5,

$$
\frac{|E_{\mathrm{exc}}|}{|E|}
\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}
<\frac1{4000}.
$$

Set

$$
k_1=\left\lfloor\frac{k}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Starting from $(X,T,E\setminus E_{\mathrm{exc}})$ and iteratively deleting every vertex whose current degree is less than $k_1$ removes fewer than

$$
k_1(|X|+t)
\le2\frac{k_1}{k}|E|
\le\frac{|E|}{50}
$$

additional edges and leaves a nonempty subgraph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $k_1$.

\qquad
B_Z(x,f(x))\ne0.
$$

researcher-0002:P9.1 [conditional; polished] Small-Component Corollary
DEPS: P1; P2; P3; P4; P5; P6; P7; P8; KTZ Lemma 3.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let

$$
\epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
$$

where $L$ is uniform among all affine lines of $\mathbb{F}_p^2$ and $x$ is uniform on $L$. Let $G_0$ be a graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1 with minimum degree at least

$$
k_0=\left\lfloor\frac{\epsilon p}{100}\right\rfloor.
$$

Let $\Lambda\ge1$, and suppose $G_0$ has a connected component of line measure $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$. Put $C_*=2^{40}$ and assume

$$
\epsilon\ge\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000C_*\Lambda}\left(\frac{d}{p}\right)^{1/2}.
$$

Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.
$$

researcher-0002:P10.1 [proved; polished] Balanced-Color Realization
DEPS: Chernoff bound
STATEMENT: Let $p\ge103$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $K$ be an integer satisfying

$$
2\le K\le\frac{p}{100\log p}.
$$

There exist a function $f:\mathbb{F}_p^2\to\mathbb{F}_p$ and a constant polynomial $P_L$ on every affine line $L$ such that, with

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)=|\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|
$$

and

$$
\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)=|\{L\ni x:P_L(x)=f(x)\}|,
$$

one has, for every affine line $L$ and every point $x$,

$$
\frac{p}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L)\le\frac{2p}{K},
$$

$$
\frac{p+1}{2K}\le\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(x)\le\frac{2(p+1)}{K}.
$$

The test agreement satisfies

$$
\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K.
$$

researcher-0002:P11.1 [proved; polished] Covering Lower Bound
DEPS: P10
STATEMENT: Let $f$ and the line table be as in P10. For a set $R$ of affine lines, define

$$
S_R=\{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2:\exists\,M\in R\text{ with }x\in M\text{ and }P_M(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose that every affine line $L$ satisfies

$$
\left|S_R\cap\{x\in L:P_L(x)=f(x)\}\right|
\ge\frac12\deg_{\mathrm{acc}}(L).
$$

Then

$$
|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon}.
$$

Moreover, there exists a constant polynomial $Q\in\mathbb{F}_p[X,Y]$ such that

$$
\Pr_{x\in\mathbb{F}_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac1K\ge\frac{\epsilon}{2}.
$$

researcher-0003:P1.1 [proved; polished] Weighted Sylvester degree
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and $d\ge1$. Give $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ the weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, where at least one of $r,s$ is positive. Then $$\deg_{X,Y}\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)\le su+rv-drs=\frac{uv}{d}-\frac{(u-dr)(v-ds)}{d}\le\frac{uv}{d}.$$

researcher-0003:P2.1 [proved; polished] Common graph-line count
DEPS: researcher-0003:P1.1
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses of the weighted Sylvester-degree lemma, let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$. For every $L\in\mathcal T$, let $q_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ have degree at most $d$ and suppose $$U(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0.$$ Then $$|\mathcal T|\le\left\lfloor\frac{uv}{d}\right\rfloor.$$

researcher-0003:P3.1 [proved; polished] Weighted graph interpolation
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, and assign to each $L\in\mathcal R$ a polynomial $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$. Define $$N_d(D)=\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dk+2}{2}.$$ If $n>D$ and $N_d(D)>n(D+1)$, then there exists a nonzero $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0\qquad(L\in\mathcal R),$$ and $A$ depends on $Z$ with $A_Z\ne0$.

researcher-0003:P4.1 [proved; polished] Primitive squarefree reduction
DEPS: researcher-0003:P3.1
STATEMENT: Let $p,d,D,\mathcal R$, and $\{P_L\}_{L\in\mathcal R}$ satisfy the hypotheses of the weighted graph-interpolation lemma. Among the nonzero polynomials satisfying all graph identities, let $A$ have minimum weighted degree. Then $A$ is $Z$-dependent and squarefree. If $A=HB$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content of $A$ as a polynomial in $Z$, then $B$ is primitive in $Z$ and squarefree. Moreover, for every $L\in\mathcal R$ with $H(L(t))\not\equiv0$, $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.$$

researcher-0003:P5.1 [proved; polished] Vertical separability
DEPS: researcher-0003:P4.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $1\le d\le D<p$. Give $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ the weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is $Z$-dependent, squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and has weighted degree at most $D$, then $$\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$

researcher-0003:P6.1 [proved; polished] Vertical ramification cleanup
DEPS: researcher-0003:P2.1; researcher-0003:P5.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $A=HB\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ have weighted degree at most $D$, where $H\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the $Z$-content and $B$ is $Z$-dependent, primitive, and squarefree. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines, each carrying $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$, such that $A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Call $L$ content-trivial when $H(L(t))\equiv0$ and identically ramified when $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Then at most $$\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$ lines in $\mathcal T$ are content-trivial or identically ramified. Every other $L\in\mathcal T$ contains at most $D-d$ values $t\in\mathbb F_p$ satisfying $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))=0$. Under uniform affine point-line sampling, deleting all these lines and points loses incidence mass at most $$\frac{D^2/d-D}{p(p+1)}+\frac{D-d}{p}.$$

researcher-0003:P7.1 [proved; polished] Component-sensitive lifting
DEPS: researcher-0003:P5.1
STATEMENT: Let $B$ be a primitive squarefree polynomial as in the vertical-separability lemma. Suppose $(\mathbf b,\alpha)\in\mathbb F_p^2\times\mathbb F_p$ satisfies $B(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$ and $B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$. Then there is a unique irreducible factor $C$ of $B$ vanishing at $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$. Set $\delta_C=\operatorname{wdeg}C$. Suppose more than $\delta_C$ distinct affine lines through $\mathbf b$, parameterized by $L(0)=\mathbf b$, carry polynomials $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfying $\deg P_L\le d$, $P_L(0)=\alpha$, and $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$$ on every supplied line and $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.$$ Moreover, $C$ is associated to $Z-Q$.

researcher-0003:P8.1 [conditional; polished] KTZ chain substitution
DEPS: researcher-0003:P3.1; researcher-0003:P6.1; KTZ Lemmas 3.1–4.3 and 6.1–6.3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, and let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ line labels $\{P_L\}$ have uniform point-line incidence agreement $\varepsilon$. Assume the hypotheses and conclusions of KTZ Lemmas 3.1–4.3 and 6.1–6.3 apply, with the weighted graph interpolation and vertical ramification cleanup used at their corresponding stages. If $\varepsilon\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ for a sufficiently large absolute constant $C$, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\varepsilon}{8000}.$$

researcher-0003:P9.1 [proved; polished] Uniform multiplicity barrier
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $D\ge d\ge101$. Let $W_D$ be the space of polynomials in $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of $(1,1,d)$-weighted degree at most $D$. Suppose ambient Hasse multiplicity $s\ge1$ is imposed at $M$ lifted points and existence is certified by $$M\binom{s+2}{3}<\dim W_D.$$ If multiplicity root counting on a target graph containing $k$ interpolated points requires $sk>D$, then $$k^3>\frac{Md}{12}.$$ In particular, if $M\ge\rho p^2$ and $k\le K\varepsilon p$, then $$\varepsilon^3>\frac{\rho}{12K^3}\frac{d}{p}.$$

researcher-0003:P10.1 [refuted; polished] Higher-Hasse lifting claim
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and $1\le d\le D<p$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive, irreducible, squarefree, and of $(1,1,d)$-weighted degree at most $D$, with $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Let $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$ satisfy $B(\mathbf b,\alpha)=B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$ and $\partial_Z^{[j]}B(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$ for some $j\ge2$. If more than $D$ distinct affine lines through $\mathbf b$, parameterized by $L(0)=\mathbf b$, carry polynomials $P_L$ with $\deg P_L\le d$, $P_L(0)=\alpha$, and $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$ on all supplied lines and $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$.

researcher-0003:P11.1 [proved; polished] Uniform-sampling exponent ceiling
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, and set $N=\binom{d+2}{2}$. If $$N\log p<(4\log4-3)p,$$ then there exist a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ labels on every affine line such that their uniform local agreement $\varepsilon$ satisfies $$\varepsilon\ge\frac{d+1}{p},$$ while every $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\Pr_{x\in\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]<\frac4p.$$ Consequently, for every fixed $\alpha>1$ and fixed positive constants $C,c$, there are arbitrarily large such instances with $\varepsilon\ge C(d/p)^\alpha$ but global agreement below $c\varepsilon$ for every total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial.

researcher-0003:P12.1 [refuted; polished] Algebraic exponent-improvement claim
DEPS: researcher-0003:P6.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d\le p-1$, let $0<\varepsilon\le1$, and let $d\le D<p$ satisfy $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\varepsilon})$. Then there exists an exponent $\alpha>1/3$ such that the hypothesis $\varepsilon\gtrsim(d/p)^\alpha$ suffices, using the vertical ramification cleanup alone, to make its surviving loss $O(\varepsilon)$.

researcher-0004:P1.1 [proved; polished] Adapted normal coordinates
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $M(t)=b_M+tv_M$ be an affine line in $\mathbb F_p^2$ with a polynomial $P_M\in\mathbb F_p[T]$ satisfying $\deg P_M\le d$. Choose $u_M\in\mathbb F_p^2$ such that $(v_M,u_M)$ is a basis. Give $T$ and $S$ weight $1$ and $W$ weight $d$. Then

$$
(X,Y)=b_M+Tv_M+Su_M,\qquad Z=P_M(T)+W
$$

defines a filtered polynomial isomorphism between $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ and $\mathbb F_p[T,S,W]$. Under this isomorphism, the defining ideal $I_M$ of the lifted graph of $P_M$ is $(S,W)$.

researcher-0004:P2.1 [proved; polished] Normal-jet codimension
DEPS: P1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $V_D\subseteq\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be the space of polynomials of weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$. For a lifted line-graph ideal $I_M$, define

$$
C_s(D,d)=\sum_{\substack{a,b\ge 0\\a+b<s}}\max\{D-a-db+1,0\}.
$$

Then $V_D\cap I_M^s$ has codimension exactly $C_s(D,d)$ in $V_D$. If $\mathcal R$ consists of $n$ distinct lifted line graphs, then the simultaneous conditions $A\in I_M^s$ for all $M\in\mathcal R$ impose at most $nC_s(D,d)$ homogeneous linear equations on $V_D$.

researcher-0004:P3.1 [proved; polished] Jet interpolation
DEPS: P2
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
N_d(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dj+2}{2}.
$$

If $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$, then there exists a nonzero polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ of weighted degree at most $D$ such that $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$.

researcher-0004:P4.1 [proved; polished] Line-factor divisibility
DEPS: P1; P3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines. Let $A\ne 0$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. For each $M\in\mathcal R$, let $\ell_M$ be a nonzero affine-linear equation defining $M$. If $A$ is independent of $Z$, then

$$
\prod_{M\in\mathcal R}\ell_M^s\mid A
$$

and $D\ge sn$. Consequently, if $D<sn$, then $A$ depends on $Z$.

researcher-0004:P4.2 [proved; polished] Primitive degree cost
DEPS: P1; P3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge 1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines. Let $A\ne0$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. If $n>D$, then

$$
\deg_Z A\ge s
$$

and $D\ge sd$.

researcher-0004:P4.3 [proved; polished] Formal derivative
DEPS: P1; P3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, and let $d\le D<p$. If a polynomial $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ has weighted degree at most $D$ for weights $(1,1,d)$ and depends on $Z$, then its formal derivative satisfies

$$
A_Z=\frac{\partial A}{\partial Z}\ne0.
$$

researcher-0004:P5.1 [proved; polished] Transverse root multiplicity
DEPS: P1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $M$ and $L$ be distinct affine lines with parameterizations $M(r)$ and $L(t)$. Let $P_M$ and $P_L$ have degree at most $d$. Suppose that

$$
L(t_0)=M(r_0),\qquad P_L(t_0)=P_M(r_0).
$$

If $A$ has weighted degree at most $D$ and $A\in I_M^s$, then

$$
(t-t_0)^s\mid A(L(t),P_L(t)).
$$

researcher-0004:P6.1 [proved; polished] Target-line transfer
DEPS: P5
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Let $A$ have weighted degree at most $D$ and satisfy $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. Let $L$ be an affine line with a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Suppose there are distinct parameters $t_1,\ldots,t_h$ and, for each $i$, a line $M_i\in\mathcal R$ with $M_i\ne L$ and a parameter $r_i$ such that

$$
L(t_i)=M_i(r_i),\qquad P_L(t_i)=P_{M_i}(r_i).
$$

If $sh>D$, then

$$
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0004:P7.1 [proved; polished] Explicit degree choice
DEPS: P2; P3; P6
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $n\ge1$ and $s\ge1$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
D_0=\left\lfloor4\sqrt{dns(s+1)}\right\rfloor+1.
$$

If $d\le D_0<p$, then there exists a nonzero polynomial $A$ of weighted degree at most $D_0$ satisfying $A\in I_M^s$ for every $M\in\mathcal R$. If, in addition, $D_0<sh$, then every target line having $h$ distinct compatible intersections with members of $\mathcal R$ satisfies

$$
A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

researcher-0004:P8.1 [proved; polished] Rank-blind barrier
DEPS: P3; P4; P6
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $100<d<p$, let $s\ge1$ and $d\le D<p$, and let $\mathcal R$ consist of $n$ distinct affine lines equipped with polynomials of degree at most $d$. Define

$$
N_d(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor D/d\rfloor}\binom{D-dj+2}{2},\qquad C_s(D,d)=\sum_{\substack{a,b\ge0\\a+b<s}}\max\{D-a-db+1,0\}.
$$

Suppose that

$$
n>D,\qquad N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d),\qquad sh>D.
$$

Then

$$
h^2>\frac{dn}{12}.
$$

If, along a parameter family with $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, one additionally has $n=\Theta(p/\varepsilon)$ and $h=\Theta(\varepsilon p)$, then

$$
\varepsilon^3=\Omega\!\left(\frac{d}{p}\right).
$$

researcher-0005:[P1].1 [proved; polished] Hasse Height
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Assign weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $C=\sum_{k\ge0}c_k(X,Y)Z^k\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent, and define

$$e:=\min\{v_p(k):k>0,\ c_k\ne0\},\qquad s:=p^e.$$

For the Hasse derivatives determined by

$$C(X,Y,Z+T)=\sum_{r\ge0}\partial_Z^{[r]}C(X,Y,Z)T^r,$$

one has $\partial_Z^{[r]}C=0$ for every $0<r<s$, while $\partial_Z^{[s]}C\ne0$, $\gcd(C,\partial_Z^{[s]}C)=1$, and $\operatorname{wdeg}C\ge ds$.

researcher-0005:[P2].1 [proved; polished] Vertical Multiplicity
DEPS: [P1]
STATEMENT: Let $B=c\prod_{i=1}^r C_i\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, where $c\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ and the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent polynomials with Hasse heights $s_i$. If an affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ and a polynomial $q(t)\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy

$$C_i(L(t),q(t))\equiv0,$$

then

$$(Z-q(t))^{s_i}\mid C_i(L(t),Z)$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t,Z]$. Moreover, $\gcd(B,B_Z)$ has exactly the irreducible factors $C_i$ for which $s_i>1$.

researcher-0005:[P3].1 [proved; polished] Weighted Resultants
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with

$$u=\operatorname{wdeg}U,\quad v=\operatorname{wdeg}V,\quad r=\deg_ZU,\quad s=\deg_ZV,$$

under the weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a set of distinct affine lines in $\mathbb F_p^2$, each equipped with one polynomial $q_L$ of degree at most $d$, such that

$$U(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t]$. Then

$$|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs\le\frac{uv}{d}.$$

researcher-0005:[P4].1 [proved; polished] Hasse Strata
DEPS: [P1]; [P3]
STATEMENT: Let $s$ be a fixed $p$-power, and let

$$G_s:=\prod_{i:s_i=s}C_i,
\qquad \Delta_s:=\operatorname{wdeg}G_s,
\qquad r_s:=\deg_ZG_s$$

for a nonempty collection of distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent factors of Hasse height $s$. For any family of affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels, the number of lines satisfying both formal identities

$$G_s(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0,
\qquad
\partial_Z^{[s]}G_s(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

is at most

$$(2r_s-s)\Delta_s-dr_s^2.$$

researcher-0005:[P5].1 [proved; polished] Content Removal
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let

$$A(X,Y,Z)=H(X,Y)B(X,Y,Z),
\qquad h:=\deg H,$$

where $H\ne0$ and $B=c\prod_{i=1}^rC_i$ with $c\in\mathbb F_p^\times$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels satisfying

$$A(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$$

in $\mathbb F_p[t]$. At most $h$ lines in $\mathcal T$ satisfy $H(L(t))\equiv0$. Every other line satisfies $B(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$ and $C_i(L(t),q_L(t))\equiv0$ for at least one $i$.

researcher-0005:[P6].1 [proved; polished] Exceptional Lines
DEPS: [P3]; [P5]
STATEMENT: Let

$$B=c\prod_{i=1}^rC_i,
\qquad \delta_i:=\operatorname{wdeg}C_i,
\qquad \Delta:=\sum_i\delta_i,$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible $Z$-dependent factors, and let

$$\iota:=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)=\sum_{C_{i,Z}=0}\delta_i.$$

For $C_{i,Z}\ne0$, set $D_iC_i:=C_{i,Z}$ and $\omega_i:=d$. For $C_{i,Z}=0$, at least one member of $\{C_{i,X},C_{i,Y}\}$ is nonzero; choose one as $D_iC_i$ and set $\omega_i:=1$.

Among the non-content graph lines supplied by [P5], call a line exceptional if either at least two distinct factors vanish identically on it, or exactly one factor $C_i$ vanishes identically and $D_iC_i$ also vanishes identically. The number $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$ of exceptional lines satisfies

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\sum_i\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i(\delta_i-\omega_i)}{d}\right\rfloor
+
\sum_{i<j}\left\lfloor\frac{\delta_i\delta_j}{d}\right\rfloor$$

and

$$N_{\mathrm{exc}}
\le
\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^2-d\Delta+(d-1)\iota}{d}\right\rfloor.$$

researcher-0005:[P7].1 [proved; polished] Root Deletion
DEPS: [P6]
STATEMENT: Retain the notation of [P6], and suppose at most $h$ content lines and $N_{\mathrm{exc}}$ exceptional lines have been removed. On each remaining line of unique type $i$, delete every parameter value at which $D_iC_i$ or any factor $C_j$ with $j\ne i$ vanishes. The number deleted from that line is at most

$$\min\{p,\Delta-\omega_i\}.$$

Thus a separable-type line loses at most $\min\{p,\Delta-d\}$ points, and an inseparable-type line loses at most $\min\{p,\Delta-1\}$ points. If $\sigma_{\mathrm{sep}}$ and $\sigma_{\mathrm{ins}}$ are the respective numbers of remaining separable- and inseparable-type lines divided by $p(p+1)$, the deleted fraction of the ambient incidence space is at most

$$\frac{h+N_{\mathrm{exc}}}{p(p+1)}
+\sigma_{\mathrm{sep}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\Delta-d}{p}\right\}
+\sigma_{\mathrm{ins}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\Delta-1}{p}\right\}.$$

Deleting all remaining point vertices of degree at most $1$ adds at most $1/(p+1)$ to this fraction.

researcher-0005:[P8].1 [proved; polished] Simple Intersections
DEPS: [P7]
STATEMENT: In the incidence system retained after [P7], let $(x,y,z)\in\mathbb F_p^3$ be incident to at least two distinct retained graph lines. Then

$$B(x,y,z)=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
B_Z(x,y,z)\ne0.$$

researcher-0005:[P9].1 [proved; polished] Automatic Separability
DEPS: [P1]; [P6]; [P7]; [P8]
STATEMENT: Let $B=c\prod_iC_i$ be squarefree and $Z$-dependent, with $\Delta=\operatorname{wdeg}B$ and $\iota=\operatorname{wdeg}\gcd(B,B_Z)$. If

$$\Delta<pd,$$

then

$$\iota=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$

If additionally $A=HB$, $h=\deg H$, $a=h+\Delta$, and

$$a\le D<pd,$$

then the cleanup removes at most

$$\left\lfloor\frac{D^2}{d}-D\right\rfloor$$

content and exceptional lines in total and at most $D-d$ parameter values from every other line. Every retained point incident to at least two retained graph lines satisfies $B_Z\ne0$.

researcher-0005:[P10].1 [proved; polished] KTZ Parameter Insertion
DEPS: [P9]; KTZ Theorem 1.1 and Lemmas 3.2–6.3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line labels $q_L$ have local agreement

$$\epsilon:=\Pr_{L\in\mathcal L,\,x\in L}[q_L(x)=f(x)].$$

Let $C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}$ be the absolute constant in the quoted KTZ theorem. If

$$\epsilon\ge C_{\mathrm{KTZ}}\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3},$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$\frac{|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x,y)=f(x,y)\}|}{p^2}\ge\frac{\epsilon}{8000}.$$

researcher-0005:[P11].1 [proved; polished] Inseparable Pencil
DEPS: [P1]
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Define

$$F(X,Y,Z):=Z^p-XY^{p-1}.$$

Then $F$ is irreducible and squarefree, $\operatorname{wdeg}F=pd$, $F_Z=0$, $\partial_Z^{[r]}F=0$ for $1\le r<p$, and $\partial_Z^{[p]}F=1$. For every $[a:b]\in\mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_p)$, set

$$L_{a,b}(t):=(at,bt),
\qquad
q_{a,b}(t):=ab^{p-1}t.$$

Then

$$F(L_{a,b}(t),Z)=(Z-q_{a,b}(t))^p.$$

Consequently, the multiplicity-weighted direction count is

$$p(p+1)>pd=\operatorname{wdeg}F,$$

but there is no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[[X,Y]]$ satisfying $F(X,Y,Q(X,Y))=0$.

researcher-0005:[P12].1 [refuted; polished] Soundness Counterexample Attempt
DEPS: [P11]
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Define

$$f(x,y):=xy^{p-1}.$$

Label every affine line other than the horizontal axis by the restriction of $X$, and label the horizontal axis by the constant polynomial $0$. Then this table and label family is a counterexample to affine line-versus-point soundness.

researcher-0005:[P13].1 [refuted; polished] Direction-Concentration Attempt
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $0\le k\le p+1$. Set $f=0$, label every line in $k$ complete direction classes by the constant polynomial $0$, and label every other affine line by the constant polynomial $1$. Then concentration of all accepted incidences in those $k$ direction classes obstructs global recovery by a total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial.

researcher-0006:P1.1 [proved; polished] Vertical separability
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. If $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in $Z$, and satisfies $\operatorname{wdeg}B<dp$, then $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$.

researcher-0006:P2.1 [proved; polished] Weighted resultant bound
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $\operatorname{wt}(X)=\operatorname{wt}(Y)=1$ and $\operatorname{wt}(Z)=d$. Let $U,V\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be nonzero and coprime, with $u=\operatorname{wdeg}U$, $v=\operatorname{wdeg}V$, $r=\deg_ZU$, and $s=\deg_ZV$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine lines with parametrizations $L(t)$ and labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ such that $U(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv V(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ for every $L\in\mathcal T$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le su+rv-drs$.

researcher-0006:P3.1 [proved; polished] Frobenius purification
DEPS: P2
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $C\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent, with $\delta=\operatorname{wdeg}C$, $r=\deg_ZC$, and exactly $k\ge2$ absolute factors. If $\mathcal T$ is a family of distinct affine $\mathbb F_p$-lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$ satisfying $C(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, then $|\mathcal T|\le(2r\delta-dr^2)/k^2\le\delta^2/(dk^2)\le\delta^2/(4d)$.

researcher-0006:P4.1 [proved; polished] Factor aggregation
DEPS: P3
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$, with weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$ and satisfy $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a family of distinct affine $\mathbb F_p$-lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels such that every $L\in\mathcal T$ is supported by an $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible, geometrically reducible factor of $B$. Then $|\mathcal T|\le D^2/(4d)$. If additionally $p>2d$, $\nu\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ is a nonsquare, and $H_0$ is a product of $d$ distinct affine-line equations, then $C=Z^2-\nu H_0^2$ is $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible with two absolute factors and supports exactly $d=(2d)^2/(4d)$ degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines.

researcher-0006:P5.1 [proved; polished] Smooth ramification sparsity
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$, let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and let $\mathcal T$ be a family of affine lines parametrized by $L(t)$ with labels $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Let $\mathcal E$ be the set of pairs $(L,\tau)$ satisfying $P_L(\tau)=f(L(\tau))$ and $B_Z(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))=0$. If every lift $(L(\tau),P_L(\tau))$ belonging to $\mathcal E$ is smooth on $B=0$, then $|\mathcal E|\le p^2$, and $|\mathcal E|/[p^2(p+1)]\le1/(p+1)$.

researcher-0006:P6.1 [proved; polished] Linear vertical regime
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $d\le D<2d$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, $Z$-dependent, and satisfy $\operatorname{wdeg}B\le D$. Then $|\{(x,y)\in\mathbb F_p^2:\exists z\in\mathbb F_p,\ B(x,y,z)=B_Z(x,y,z)=0\}|\le D(D-d)$, and there is no affine-line parametrization $L(t)$ with a degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $P(t)$ satisfying both $B(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$ and $B_Z(L(t),P(t))\equiv0$.

researcher-0006:P7.1 [proved; polished] Singular construction algebra
DEPS: P1; P6
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d\le p-2$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, and set $D=d+1$, $s=\lfloor(d+1)/2\rfloor$, and $e=d+1-2s\in\{0,1\}$. Choose distinct $c_1,\ldots,c_s\in\mathbb F_p$. If $e=1$, choose $b\in\mathbb F_p\setminus\{c_1,\ldots,c_s\}$, and define $H(Y)=\prod_{j=1}^s(Y-c_j)^2(Y-b)^e$ and $B(X,Y,Z)=XZ+H(Y)$, with the last factor omitted when $e=0$. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, and absolutely irreducible, with $\operatorname{wdeg}B=D<p$, $B_Z=X$, and $\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. Its normalized degree-one discriminant is $1$, while $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=X$, whose zero set is a smooth absolutely irreducible affine line. For every $1\le j\le s$, the point $(0,c_j,0)$ is singular on $B=0$.

researcher-0006:P8.1 [proved; polished] Explicit incidence table
DEPS: P7
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of [P7], for $1\le j\le s$ and $m\in\mathbb F_p$ define $L_{j,m}(t)=(t,c_j+mt)$ and $P_{j,m}(t)=-H(c_j+mt)/t$. Define $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ by $f(x,y)=-H(y)/x$ when $x\ne0$ and $f(0,y)=0$, and give every unselected affine line the constant label $0$. Then every $P_{j,m}$ is a polynomial of degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))\equiv0$, and every one of the $sp>D$ selected lines is fully accepted by $f$. Their uniform line measure is $s/(p+1)$, so the table's agreement is at least $s/(p+1)$. Moreover, $B_Z(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))=t$, so each selected line has exactly one ramified accepted parameter, and the normalized mass of the selected ramified incidences is $s/[p(p+1)]$.

researcher-0006:P9.1 [proved; polished] Interpolation minimality
DEPS: P8
STATEMENT: Under the hypotheses and notation of [P8], there is no polynomial $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $Q(L_{j,m}(t))\equiv P_{j,m}(t)$ for every $1\le j\le s$ and $m\in\mathbb F_p$. Moreover, every nonzero $A\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ satisfying $A(L_{j,m}(t),P_{j,m}(t))\equiv0$ for all selected lines has weighted degree at least $D=d+1$. Consequently, $B=XZ+H(Y)$ is a minimum-weight graph interpolant for the selected graph lines.

researcher-0006:P10.1 [proved; polished] Monic stress test
DEPS: P5
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $2d\le D<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, set $r=D-d$, choose distinct $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb F_p$, and define $A(X)=\prod_{i=1}^r(X-a_i)$ and $B_{\mathrm{sm}}(X,Y,Z)=Z^2+A(X)Z+Y$. Then $B_{\mathrm{sm}}$ is monic in $Z$, has weighted degree $D$, and is smooth and absolutely irreducible. Its $Z$-discriminant $\Delta_{\mathrm{sm}}(X,Y)=A(X)^2-4Y$ is also smooth and absolutely irreducible. The label $P_L(t)=0$ on the line $L(t)=(t,0)$ satisfies $B_{\mathrm{sm}}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$, and $(B_{\mathrm{sm}})_Z(L(t),P_L(t))=A(t)$ has exactly $D-d$ distinct roots.

researcher-0006:P11.1 [proved; polished] Dense singular pencil
DEPS: P1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $d\ge101$, and assume $p>4d+1$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$, set $D=2d$, choose distinct nonzero $a_1,\ldots,a_{d-1}\in\mathbb F_p$, and define $g(X)=\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}(X-a_i)$ and $B_{\mathrm{den}}(X,Y,Z)=Z^2-XYg(X)^2$. Then $B_{\mathrm{den}}$ has weighted degree $D$ and is absolutely irreducible. For every nonzero square $s\in\mathbb F_p$, choose $c_s\in\mathbb F_p^\times$ with $c_s^2=s$ and set $L_s(t)=(t,st)$ and $P_s(t)=c_stg(t)$. Each $P_s$ has degree $d$ and satisfies $B_{\mathrm{den}}(L_s(t),P_s(t))\equiv0$. There are $(p-1)/2>D$ such graph lines. On every one, $(B_{\mathrm{den}})_Z$ vanishes at exactly the $d=D-d$ parameters $0,a_1,\ldots,a_{d-1}$, and every corresponding lift is singular on $B_{\mathrm{den}}=0$. There is no $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ satisfying $B_{\mathrm{den}}(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$.

researcher-0006:P12.1 [refuted; polished] Simplicity substitutes
DEPS: P7; P8; P9; P10; P11
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and give $X,Y,Z$ weights $(1,1,d)$. The following assertions hold:

1. If an absolutely irreducible graph interpolant of weighted degree at most $D<p$ supports more than $D$ distinct affine graph lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels, then it has a polynomial root $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $B(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0$, without any simple-common-lift hypothesis.

2. Smooth absolute irreducibility of the raw-resultant curve $\operatorname{Res}_Z(B,B_Z)=0$ controls the number of graph directions meeting a ramified lifted point.

3. If $2d\le D<p$, a graph interpolant is monic in $Z$, and its $Z$-discriminant is smooth and absolutely irreducible, then every degree-at-most-$d$ graph line contains $o(D-d)$ parameters at which $B_Z$ vanishes.

researcher-0006:P13.1 [conditional; polished] Singular deletion scale
DEPS: P3; P5; P12; Kominers–Thaler–Zheng Lemmas 3.2 and 4.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, let $101\le d<p$, and let $0<\epsilon\le1$. Suppose the interpolation weight satisfies $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$. Suppose a deletion argument assigns supported graph lines to fixed $\mathbb F_p$-irreducible factors, deletes graph lines supported on geometrically reducible factors using [P3], applies [P5] only to ramified lifts known to be smooth on their assigned factor, and charges every remaining singular ramified incidence by the per-line budget $D/p$. Then the normalized purification loss is $O(1/(\epsilon p))$, the normalized smooth-ramification loss for each fixed assigned factor is at most $1/(p+1)$, and the singular-ramification loss is $O(D/p)=O(\sqrt{d/(\epsilon p)})$. Requiring the last loss to be $O(\epsilon)$ imposes $\epsilon^3=\Omega(d/p)$.

researcher-0007:P1.1 [proved; polished] Factor separability
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, and write

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i,
\qquad c\in\mathbb F_p^\times,
$$

where the $C_i$ are distinct irreducible polynomials. For

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\qquad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
$$

every $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ satisfies

$$
r_i\ge1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1.
$$

researcher-0007:P2.1 [proved; polished] Weighted resultant degree
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let

$$
U=\sum_{a=0}^{r}u_a(X,Y)Z^a,
\qquad
V=\sum_{b=0}^{s}v_b(X,Y)Z^b
$$

be nonzero polynomials in $\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ satisfying

$$
\deg_Z(U)=r,
\qquad
\deg_Z(V)=s,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(U)=u,
\qquad
\operatorname{wdeg}(V)=v.
$$

With the convention $\deg_{X,Y}(0)=-\infty$,

$$
\deg_{X,Y}\operatorname{Res}_Z(U,V)\le su+rv-drs.
$$

researcher-0007:P3.1 [proved; polished] Exceptional graph lines
DEPS: researcher-0007:P1.1; researcher-0007:P2.1
STATEMENT: Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P1.1. Fix $i$ and put

$$
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
\qquad
R_i=\operatorname{Res}_Z(C_i,C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T_i$ be a family indexed by distinct affine lines with equations $\ell_L(X,Y)=0$. For each $L\in\mathcal T_i$, let $L(t)=(x_L(t),y_L(t))$ be an affine-linear parametrization and let $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfy $\deg P_L\le d$ and

$$
C_i(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0.
$$

Define

$$
\mathcal E_i=
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
$$

and, for $L\in\mathcal E_i$,

$$
\nu_L=\operatorname{ord}_{\ell_L}(R_i).
$$

Then

$$
R_i\ne0,
\qquad
\deg_{X,Y}(R_i)\le\Xi_i,
\qquad
\sum_{L\in\mathcal E_i}\nu_L\le\Xi_i.
$$

In particular,

$$
|\mathcal E_i|\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}.
$$

researcher-0007:P4.1 [proved; polished] Finite polar intersections
DEPS: researcher-0007:P1.1
STATEMENT: Let the objects satisfy the hypotheses of researcher-0007:P3.1. For $L\in\mathcal T_i\setminus\mathcal E_i$, set

$$
G_{i,L}(t)=C_{i,Z}(x_L(t),y_L(t),P_L(t)).
$$

Then $G_{i,L}\ne0$ and

$$
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb F_p}
\operatorname{ord}_{t=\alpha}G_{i,L}(t)
\le\gamma_i.
$$

researcher-0007:P5.1 [proved; polished] Factorwise cleanup bounds
DEPS: researcher-0007:P3.1; researcher-0007:P4.1
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime and let $101\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. Let $B\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be primitive in $Z$, squarefree, of positive $Z$-degree and weighted degree $\delta<p$, with

$$
B=c\prod_{i=1}^{s}C_i.
$$

For each $i$, put

$$
\delta_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),
\quad
r_i=\deg_Z(C_i),
\quad
\gamma_i=\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\delta_i+r_i\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).
$$

Let $\mathcal T=\bigsqcup_i\mathcal T_i$ be a disjoint assignment of distinct affine lines with degree-at-most-$d$ labels $P_L$, satisfying

$$
C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\qquad(L\in\mathcal T_i).
$$

For each $i$, let

$$
e_i=
\left|
\left\{L\in\mathcal T_i:
C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0
\right\}
\right|.
$$

For a point table $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, let $i(L)=i$ when $L\in\mathcal T_i$, and define

$$
\mathcal R=
\left\{(L,t):
\begin{array}{l}
L\in\mathcal T,\ t\in\mathbb F_p,\\
P_L(t)=f(L(t)),\\
C_{i(L),Z}(L(t),f(L(t)))=0
\end{array}
\right\}.
$$

Then

$$
e_i\le\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\},
$$

$$
|\mathcal R|
\le
\sum_i\left(pe_i+(|\mathcal T_i|-e_i)\gamma_i\right),
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\sum_i\frac{\min\{|\mathcal T_i|,\Xi_i\}}{p(p+1)}
+
\sum_i\frac{|\mathcal T_i|\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.
$$

Moreover,

$$
\gamma_i\le\delta_i-d
$$

and

$$
\Xi_i\le(2r_i-1)\delta_i-dr_i^2
\le\frac{\delta_i^2}{d}-\delta_i.
$$

If

$$
\tau_i=\frac{|\mathcal T_i|}{p(p+1)},
\qquad
\tau=\sum_i\tau_i>0,
\qquad
\bar\delta=\frac{\sum_i\tau_i\delta_i}{\tau},
$$

then

$$
\frac{|\mathcal R|}{p^2(p+1)}
\le
\frac{\delta^2/d-\delta}{p(p+1)}
+
\frac{\tau(\bar\delta-d)}p.
$$

researcher-0007:P6.1 [conditional; polished] Cleaned factor component
DEPS: researcher-0007:P1.1; researcher-0007:P5.1; KTZ Lemmas 2.1, 2.7, and 6.1–6.3
STATEMENT: Assume the hypotheses and notation of researcher-0007:P5.1, and fix $i$. Let $G=(\mathcal X,T',E')$ be a connected bipartite graph with $\mathcal X\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2$, $T'\subseteq\mathcal T_i$, and

$$
E'\subseteq
\{(x,L)\in\mathcal X\times T':x\in L,\ P_L(x)=f(x)\}.
$$

Suppose $G$ has minimum degree at least the integer $k$, and suppose

$$
C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\ne0
\qquad((x,L)\in E').
$$

If

$$
k>\delta_i
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\frac1{\sqrt{p+1}}\le\frac{k}{4p},
$$

then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that

$$
Q(x)=f(x)
\qquad(x\in\mathcal X)
$$

and

$$
\frac{|\{x\in\mathbb F_p^2:Q(x)=f(x)\}|}{p^2}
\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
$$

researcher-0007:P7.1 [proved; polished] Strip construction
DEPS: 
STATEMENT: Let $p$ be prime, and let $d,D$ be integers satisfying

$$
101\le d,
\qquad
2d\le D<p.
$$

Put $e=D-d$, choose $S\subseteq\mathbb F_p$ with $|S|=e$, and define

$$
H(X)=\prod_{a\in S}(X-a),
\qquad
B=Z(Z-H(X)).
$$

Define $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$ by

$$
f(x,y)=
\begin{cases}
0,&x\in S,\\
1,&x\notin S,
\end{cases}
$$

and assign the constant label $P_L=0$ to every nonvertical affine line and the constant label $P_L=2$ to every vertical affine line. Then $B$ is primitive in $Z$, squarefree, and has weighted degree $D$. Every nonvertical line has exactly $e$ accepted points and satisfies

$$
B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0,
$$

while every vertical line has no accepted points. The test agreement is

$$
\varepsilon=\frac{e}{p+1}=\frac{D-d}{p+1}.
$$

researcher-0007:P8.1 [proved; polished] Polar-loss saturation
DEPS: researcher-0007:P7.1
STATEMENT: Let $p,d,D,e,S,H,B,f$ and the line labels be as in researcher-0007:P7.1, and put

$$
\tau=\frac{p}{p+1}.
$$

On every nonvertical zero-labelled graph, $B_Z(L(t),0)$ has exactly $D-d$ simple roots, and these roots are exactly the accepted parameters. The whole-relation ramified-incidence measure is

$$
\frac{D-d}{p+1}
=\frac{\tau(D-d)}p
=\varepsilon.
$$

Assigning every supported graph to the factor $C=Z$ gives $C_Z=1$ and factorwise polar loss $0$. The polynomial $Q=0$ has total degree $0$ and global agreement

$$
\frac{D-d}{p}>\varepsilon.
$$

\frac12\left(\frac dp\right)^{1/3}

$$
D=\Theta(\varepsilon p)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
D=\Theta\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{dp}{\varepsilon}}\right).
$$

 succeeded in 0ms:
Let $B$ be a primitive squarefree polynomial as in the vertical-separability lemma. Suppose $(\mathbf b,\alpha)\in\mathbb F_p^2\times\mathbb F_p$ satisfies $B(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$ and $B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$. Then there is a unique irreducible factor $C$ of $B$ vanishing at $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$. Set $\delta_C=\operatorname{wdeg}C$. Suppose more than $\delta_C$ distinct affine lines through $\mathbf b$, parameterized by $L(0)=\mathbf b$, carry polynomials $P_L\in\mathbb F_p[t]$ satisfying $\deg P_L\le d$, $P_L(0)=\alpha$, and $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. Then there exists $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$$ on every supplied line and $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\equiv0.$$ Moreover, $C$ is associated to $Z-Q$.
PROOF
At a point where $B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$, two distinct irreducible factors of the squarefree polynomial $B$ cannot both vanish, since their simultaneous vanishing would force $B_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)=0$. Thus exactly one factor $C$ vanishes, and $C_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)\ne0$. For each supplied graph, the factorization of $B(L(t),P_L(t))$ in the domain $\mathbb F_p[t]$ forces some irreducible factor to vanish identically. Evaluation at $t=0$ shows that this factor must be $C$. Newton recursion at $(\mathbf b,\alpha)$ produces the unique total-degree-at-most-$d$ truncation $Q$ through $\alpha$ and divides only by the nonzero field element $C_Z(\mathbf b,\alpha)$. Uniqueness makes $Q(L(t))=P_L(t)$ on every supplied line. The polynomial $C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))$ has total degree at most $\delta_C$ and vanishes identically on more than $\delta_C$ distinct lines, so it is zero. Finally, the kernel of substitution $Z\mapsto Q(X,Y)$ is $(Z-Q)$; irreducibility therefore makes $C$ associated to $Z-Q$.
